**Two real, adjacent gaps the same investigation surfaced, deliberately not addressed in this part**: host/per-container pressure-stall (PSI: `cpu.pressure`/`io.pressure`/`memory.pressure`) stats, and live-tailing a running build's own output from a console session rather than only seeing captured text after failure (both queued as their own future work, the latter explicitly deferred by the user -- "we'll address it much later").

## Part 17 (done): host-wide stats endpoint (`GET /v1/system/stats`, ADR-0073)

Raised directly by the user while watching the box's own memory behave oddly during the live `lldap` build on 192.168.15.95: "we should add a system load and memory and network and so on (same as we did for containers) but for the host, right?" -- flagged explicitly as "asap" priority, since it was wanted to help monitor the ongoing deployment work itself.

New `daemon/src/main.c` handler mirroring `handle_container_stats()`'s own established shape (nested per-category JSON objects, raw cumulative counters only -- never a pre-computed rate, client does its own delta math): `load` from `/proc/loadavg`, `cpu` from `/proc/stat`'s own first `cpu` line (user/nice/system/idle/iowait/irq/softirq/steal jiffies), `memory` from `/proc/meminfo` (total/free/available/buffers/cached/swap, kB converted to bytes), `disk` from `statvfs()` on `g_base_dir`, `networks` enumerating every real interface under `/sys/class/net`. `read_net_stat()` (previously container-stats-only, veth-specific) generalized to accept any interface name and shared by both -- avoiding a second, near-identical `/sys/class/net/<if>/statistics/<file>` reader.

New `jw_num()` JSON-writer primitive (`daemon/src/json.c`/`.h`) -- load averages are the first genuinely fractional value this daemon has ever needed to serialize over JSON; every prior numeric field across the whole API was a whole-number counter or size. Fixed `%.2f` formatting, not a general-purpose float writer -- the only values ever passed through it are non-negative load averages.

`kanxeoctl host-stats` (a bare top-level verb, not `stats` -- already `stats NAME` for a container -- consistent with `swap`/`routes`/`logs`'s own flat naming for other `/v1/system/*` resources). Documented in `openapi.yaml`, `docs/api/README.md`, `docs/guides/cli-reference.md`.

Verified against a real running daemon (fresh `--data-dir=`, real `/proc`/`statvfs()` data returned, including real leftover veth/bridge interfaces from this same dev sandbox's own prior test runs -- confirming the enumeration is genuinely live, not stubbed).

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); regression sweep (`test_daemon`, `test_container_stats` -- confirming the `read_net_stat()` generalization didn't regress container stats -- plus `test_routes`/`test_system_backup` for nearby dispatch-table safety, all passing, `sudo`/unsandboxed).

**Does not address** per-container/host PSI (pressure-stall) stats -- a real, related, but separate gap, tracked as task #677.

## Part 18 (done): pressure-stall information (PSI) in host and per-container stats (ADR-0074)

Direct follow-up to Part 17, requested by the user in the same breath as host stats itself: "we want diskio cpu pressure stall, io pressure stall, and memory pressure stall graphs per container? Also for the host as we discussed?" -- raw usage counters answer "how much" but not "is anything actually stalled waiting," which is the real diagnostic question PSI answers.

New `cgroup_read_pressure()` (`src/cgroup.c`/`include/container.h`), modeled on the existing `cgroup_read_stat_key()`/`cgroup_read_io_totals()` readers: parses one cgroup v2 PSI file's `some`/`full` lines (`avg10`/`avg60`/`avg300` percentages, `total` cumulative stalled microseconds) into a `struct cgroup_pressure`. Same best-effort convention as every other reader here -- zeroed, not an error, on a kernel without `CONFIG_PSI`.

Wired into both stats endpoints via a shared `write_pressure_json()` helper (`daemon/src/main.c`), so the `{"some":{...},"full":{...}}` shape is defined once: `GET /v1/containers/{name}/stats` gains `cpu.pressure`/`memory.pressure` (nested in the existing objects) and `disk.pressure` (mapping to `io.pressure`, kept inside the existing `disk` object rather than a new top-level key); `GET /v1/system/stats` gains the same three, read from the cgroup v2 root (`/sys/fs/cgroup`, opened fresh per request) rather than any single container's leaf.

`kanxeoctl stats NAME` and `kanxeoctl host-stats` both print one summary pressure line per resource via a shared `print_pressure_line()` CLI helper. Documented in `openapi.yaml` (new reusable `Pressure`/`PressureLine` schemas, referenced from both `SystemStats` and `ContainerStats`), `docs/api/README.md`, `docs/guides/cli-reference.md`.

Verified live: host-level PSI confirmed nonzero and plausible against a real running daemon (this build sandbox's own CPU pressure genuinely elevated during the concurrent `lldap` build on 192.168.15.95); per-container wiring verified via the existing `test_container_stats` regression test passing end-to-end (the reader itself independently verified live against the real root cgroup; container leaf cgroups expose the identical file set).

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); regression sweep (`test_daemon`, `test_container_stats`, `test_routes`, all passing, `sudo`/unsandboxed).

## Part 19 (done): ICMP reachability endpoint (GET/POST /v1/system/ping, ADR-0075)

Raised while diagnosing 192.168.15.95's own real network limitations: the daemon had no way to answer "is there a route to X" independent of DNS, every prior diagnosis relying on ad-hoc, throwaway tricks.

New `daemon/src/ping.c`/`include/ping.h`: a real, hand-rolled ICMP echo (`SOCK_RAW`/`IPPROTO_ICMP`, RFC 1071 checksum, `sendto()`/`recvfrom()` matched by echo identifier+sequence -- a raw ICMP socket sees every ICMP packet system-wide, not just this job's own). No shelling out to a `ping` binary (this project's images don't have one; every other network primitive already talks to the kernel directly). `CAP_NET_RAW` is required but not a new constraint -- `kanxeod` already runs as root on any real `--init-mode` host.

Async, epoll-integrated (`CONN_PING`/`CONN_PING_TIMER`), same "kick off + poll" shape as every other uncertain-duration job here: `POST /v1/system/ping {"host":"A.B.C.D"}` starts it, `GET` polls. Two fds are genuinely in flight for one job (the socket, the timeout timer) -- whichever fires first tears down both, reusing the exact `CONN_DEAD`/`queue_conn_free()` batch-safety mechanism `console_session_teardown()` already established for the WS/PTY console pairing, rather than re-solving the same hazard. v1 single-job constraint, matching every other async job. `kanxeoctl ping HOST` polls to completion and exits nonzero on an unreachable result.

Verified live: a real loopback ping returns a genuine sub-millisecond RTT; a real unreachable target (`192.0.2.1`, RFC 5737 TEST-NET-1) times out cleanly at the fixed ~2s bound, both through the full async path and via `kanxeoctl ping`'s own exit code.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); regression sweep (`test_daemon`, `test_cli`, `test_container_stats`, `test_routes`, `test_system_backup`, `test_daemon_net`, all passing, `sudo`/unsandboxed).

## Part 20 (done): Host DNS resolver config (GET/PUT /v1/system/resolv, ADR-0076)

Raised in the same investigation as Part 19: a real installed Kanxeo host has no outbound DNS resolution mechanism at all (`kanxeod`'s own `curl` subprocess fails immediately against any real hostname), worked around repeatedly with an ad-hoc LAN-mirror trick rather than a real fix. Confirmed directly that patching `curl` itself isn't viable (this project's staged `curl` has no `c-ares`, so no `--dns-servers`; glibc's own resolver has `/etc/resolv.conf` hardcoded at compile time) -- fixing the one canonical path benefits every current and future host-level tool for free instead of patching each one individually.

New `daemon/src/resolv.c`/`include/resolv.h`: a small, dedicated module (matching this project's own "one job per file" precedent -- `swap.c`, `logstore.c`, `diskrole.c`) persisting up to `RESOLV_MAX_NAMESERVERS` (3, matching glibc's own `MAXNS`) at `<g_base_dir>/resolv.conf` in literal `nameserver A.B.C.D` format -- the same "the persisted file IS the served file" convention `dns_write_hosts_file()` already established, not JSON-wrapped. `GET`/`PUT /v1/system/resolv` is a real, first-class REST resource alongside every other host-config one (`daemon_config.json`, `site_config.json`, disk roles) -- raised directly by the user pushing back on an earlier draft that special-cased this as boot-path-only state with no persisted, API-visible home.

`boot_init()` now bind-mounts `<g_base_dir>/resolv.conf` onto `/etc/resolv.conf` once at real `--init-mode` boot, right after `g_base_dir` itself is mounted -- best-effort, a failed bind mount leaves the host at today's status quo rather than blocking boot. The control-plane squashfs has no `/etc` at all otherwise (confirmed by tracing every write path in `mkbootroot.c`), so `mkbootroot.c` now stages an empty placeholder file at `etc/resolv.conf` for `MS_BIND` to target. `PUT` writes straight to the original `g_base_dir` path (never through the bind-mounted alias), so a config change takes effect immediately with no reboot needed and no rename-onto-a-mountpoint subtlety -- only the very first activation needs the boot-time mount.

`dnsmasq.recipe`'s documented container invocation gains real upstream forwarders (`--server=1.1.1.1 --server=8.8.8.8`) -- previously purely authoritative for `.internal`, no recursion for anything else. Also documented, as an already-existing pattern requiring no new code: a container can already pull the full DNS record set itself via `GET /v1/dns/records` and reformat it however its own server software needs.

`kanxeoctl resolv [show]` / `resolv set [--nameserver=A.B.C.D ...]` (repeatable flag, up to 3; no flags clears it).

`boot_init()`'s new bind-mount code only runs under real `--init-mode` -- confirmed inert for this project's entire test suite (no test invokes that flag) -- real verification is necessarily live, on an actual reboot, not unit-testable in this sandbox. Live-verified everything short of that: `kanxeoctl resolv`/`resolv set` against a real running daemon (empty -> set -> persisted, matching output).

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); regression sweep (`test_daemon`, `test_cli`, `test_container_stats`, `test_routes`, `test_system_backup`, `test_daemon_net`, all passing, `sudo`/unsandboxed).

## Part 21 (done): Split GET /health into liveness + GET /system/boot identity (ADR-0077)

`GET /health` originally answered two different questions at once: "is the daemon up" and "which build/slot is this" (`build_version`/`build_time`/`slot`, added earlier for A/B update verification). Both `kanxeoctl` and the web dashboard poll `/health` every few seconds purely for a status dot -- conflating that cheap liveness ping with a build/slot identity snapshot was the wrong shape, especially with a new `kernel_version` field (from `uname(2)`) wanting a home alongside the existing identity fields rather than bolted onto the polling path.

`handle_health()` reverts to `{"status": "ok"}` only. New `handle_system_boot()`/`GET /system/boot` carries `build_version`/`build_time`/`slot` (unchanged) plus `kernel_version` (new, read fresh via `uname(2)` on every call, not cached at startup). `kanxeoctl boot` is the CLI surface; `kanxeoctl health` drops its `build:`/`slot:` output lines to match the now-minimal response.

Every place that referenced `/health`'s build/slot fields for deploy verification now points at `/system/boot` instead: `docs/guides/kernel-build-and-ab-updates.md`'s "confirm it stuck" section, `docs/guides/remote-development.md`'s deploy-confirmation walkthrough, `docs/api/README.md`'s new "Liveness vs. boot identity" section.

Live-verified against a real running daemon: `kanxeoctl health --json` now returns just `{"status":"ok"}`; `kanxeoctl boot --json` returns `build_version`/`build_time`/`slot`/`kernel_version` (confirmed `kernel_version` matches the sandbox's real running kernel via `uname -r`).

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); regression sweep (`test_daemon`, `test_cli`, `test_container_stats`, `test_routes`, `test_system_backup`, `test_daemon_net`, all passing, `sudo`/unsandboxed).

## Part 22 (done): From-source host tools bootstrap (ADR-0078)

Raised directly by the user reviewing `image/src/mkbootroot.c`'s own `shelled_bins[]` table: every binary `kanxeod` shells out to at runtime (`openssl`/`curl`/`tar`/`gzip`/`bzip2`/`xz`/`cp`/`rm`/`sha256sum`/`unsquashfs`/`mkfs.ext4`) was a raw copy of whatever the dev sandbox running `mkbootroot` happened to have installed -- not "from source" for a squashfs meant to run on someone else's hardware, and a direct contradiction of this project's own "completely atomic OS distribution" goal.

`mkbootroot.c` gains a new, optional `host_tools_dir` argument (the same tolerant-default shape `firmware_dir`/`modules_dir`/`kmod_bin_dir` already established) -- when given, `cp`/`rm`/`sha256sum`/`gzip` (the four tools this project already had real from-source recipes for, `coreutils.recipe`/`gzip.recipe`) are sourced from there instead of the dev host. Seven new recipes close the rest of the real gap: `openssl.recipe` (retires `openssl-dev.recipe`'s own dev-host-copy stopgap -- `install_sw`+`install_ssldirs`, confirmed via a real local build+install+`openssl req -x509` round trip), `curl.recipe` (deliberately narrow -- OpenSSL+zlib only, no HTTP/2/IDN/SSH/RTMP/LDAP/GSSAPI/Brotli/Zstd, none of which is real confirmed need for `pkg.c`'s own plain `-fsSL ... -o <path> <URL>` usage), `tar.recipe`, `bzip2.recipe` (a real `make clean` between its static and shared-lib Makefile targets required, confirmed the hard way), `xz.recipe`, `squashfs-tools.recipe` (built with only `XZ_SUPPORT=1` -- a direct grep of this project's own `-comp` flags confirms xz is the only format ever produced), and `e2fsprogs.recipe` (scoped to `mke2fs`/`mkfs.ext4` only, the confirmed real need).

Every recipe empirically verified via a real local build before being trusted: `openssl` (a real self-signed cert generated), `curl` (a real HTTPS fetch of a live URL with the exact flags `pkg.c` itself uses), `tar`/`bzip2`/`xz` (real compress+decompress round trips), `squashfs-tools` (a real `mksquashfs`+`unsquashfs` round trip), `e2fsprogs` (a real `mkfs.ext4` against a scratch file, filesystem actually created). Checksums cross-verified against a second independent source for every one (a second mirror, a Debian orig tarball, or -- where GitHub is the only real distribution point, `squashfs-tools` -- content-identical via a real `diff -r` of both extracted trees, since GitHub's own tag archives aren't byte-reproducible against a maintainer's release or downstream repackaging even for identical source).

A new well-known image name, `kanxeo-hosttools` (`HOST_TOOLS_IMAGE` in `daemon/src/main.c`, built exactly like `kanxeo-builder`/`dev` -- `docs/guides/building-kanxeo.md`'s new "Host tools image" section) -- `spawn_kanxeo_bootroot_assembly()` checks for its rootfs and passes it as `host_tools_dir` when present, `""` (today's dev-host-sourced behavior) on any box that never builds it. The `host_tools_dir` mechanism itself live-verified two ways: a synthetic fake tools directory (distinguishable shell-script stand-ins) confirmed `cp`/`rm`/`sha256sum`/`gzip` are genuinely sourced from wherever this argument points, while `openssl` (not yet wired) still comes from the dev host's own real ELF binary; a real `pkg install` of `tar.recipe` through the full daemon pipeline (fetch -> checksum verify -> isolated build container -> DESTDIR install) confirmed the recipe format itself is correct end-to-end.

**Deliberately not yet done, tracked explicitly rather than silently dropped** (ADR-0078's own "Consequences" section): `mkbootroot.c`'s `shelled_bins[]` table isn't repointed at `host_tools_dir` for the other seven tools (`openssl`/`curl`/`tar`/`bzip2`/`xz`/`unsquashfs`/`mkfs.ext4`) yet -- mechanical once done for the first four, the same argument threads through cleanly, but a real follow-on. A full live `kanxeo-hosttools` build (all seven new recipes actually `pkg install`ed onto a real image) is folded into the final validation pass on 192.168.15.95 rather than repeated here first -- that box is the real target this bootstrap exists for.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); regression sweep (`test_daemon`, `test_cli`, `test_container_stats`, `test_routes`, `test_system_backup`, `test_daemon_net`, all passing, `sudo`/unsandboxed).

## Part 23 (done): Final live validation on 192.168.15.95 -- ping/resolv/boot/PSI confirmed, kanxeo bootroot assembly gets real REST-visible diagnostics

Closed out this session's own master directive: rebuild+redeploy the full ADR-0075..0078 body of work onto the real target box, reboot into it, and confirm every new endpoint live rather than trusting local verification alone.

Deploy itself surfaced a second real instance of exactly the class of gap ADR-0078 already named as a known follow-on: `pkg hostbuild kanxeo` refused a second run with a bare `409` (task #668, no upgrade/force path -- worked around by `pkg rm kanxeo@__hostbuild` first) and, once running, the box's own automatic bootroot assembly (ADR-0057) failed with zero visible signal (task #669) -- confirmed the hard way to be `spawn_kanxeo_bootroot_assembly()`/`handle_bootroot_assemble_event()` only ever reporting via `fprintf(stderr, ...)`, invisible to `GET /system/logs` and therefore to any operator without serial console access. Root cause of that specific failure: the box's own still-running (pre-session) `kanxeod` called a freshly-hostbuilt `mkbootroot` (already rebuilt from this session's own source, which requires the new 10th `host_tools_dir` argument, task #687) with the old 9-argument convention -- a real, live demonstration of why the diagnostic gap mattered.

Fixed both the diagnostic gap (`logstore_write()` added alongside every existing `fprintf(stderr, ...)` in `spawn_kanxeo_bootroot_assembly()`/`handle_bootroot_assemble_event()`, including real exit-code/signal detail, not just pass/fail) and, for this one deploy, sidestepped the version-mismatch bootstrap catch-22 by building `kanxeod-root.squashfs` locally (self-consistent: `kanxeod`/`kanxeoctl`/`mkbootroot` all from the same commit) and pushing it via the established LAN-serve + hostbuild-artifact trick (`docs/guides/remote-development.md`) so `POST /system/update` could target a real host path. Reboot succeeded; the new build (`kanxeod`/`kanxeoctl` genuinely running on slot b) confirmed via `GET /system/boot`.

Live-verified against the real running daemon: `GET /health` returns just `{"status":"ok"}`; `GET /system/resolv` returns `{"nameservers":[]}`; a real ICMP `POST /system/ping {"host":"127.0.0.1"}` resolved `reachable:true` with a genuine sub-millisecond RTT; `GET /system/stats` returns real, populated `cpu`/`memory`/`disk` PSI (`pressure.some`/`pressure.full`); the renamed `address`/`has_address` network fields (Part 5, an earlier phase) still correct post-reboot. A second `pkg hostbuild kanxeo` cycle, run entirely on the now-self-consistent new build, confirmed the diagnostic fix works for real: `GET /system/logs` showed a genuine `kanxeo bootroot assembly: mkbootroot exited 1` line -- REST-visible for the first time, closing task #669's own real gap, even though the assembly itself still fails for a reason not yet root-caused (a real, separate, newly-discovered issue, not this session's own scope -- see below).

**A real, separate, newly-discovered regression, explicitly not resolved this session**: container creation with *any* resource-limit flag (`--network=`, `--memory-max=`, `--pids-max=`) fails with a bare `500` on this box post-reboot, while a bare `run --image=base -- ...` with no flags succeeds -- confirmed reproducible three separate ways, and confirmed via `GET /system/logs` that container-creation failures aren't logged to the log store at all (a genuinely different, not-yet-tracked silent-failure gap, the same class as #669 but for `handle_container_create()`, not `pkg.c`). Not caused by anything touched this session (no container/cgroup/network-attach code was changed) -- most likely a pre-existing or environmental condition on this specific box, first surfaced here because this is the first time this session actually tried a resource-limited container post-reboot. This blocks the long-pending lldap/LDAPS closure (tasks #623/#624) and container-level PSI verification -- both queued, not silently dropped, pending root-cause on this real regression.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings) prior to deploy; regression sweep all passing.

## Part 24 (done): cgroup v2 controller delegation fix -- root cause and fix for the real-PID-1 container-create 500 regression (ADR-0079)

Direct follow-on to Part 23's own newly-discovered regression, per explicit user instruction to dig into it.

Root cause, confirmed by reading `cgroup_create()` (`src/cgroup.c`) against real cgroup v2 kernel semantics and reproducing locally: `cgroup.subtree_control` starts genuinely empty at every level by kernel default -- no controller is ever auto-enabled for child cgroups, regardless of what `cgroup.controllers` lists as merely *available*. A parent must explicitly write `+<controller>` to its own `subtree_control` before that controller's files (`memory.max`, `pids.max`, etc.) exist in any child at all; creating the bare child cgroup directory needs no delegated controller, which is exactly why flagless containers were unaffected. Every dev/test environment this project had exercised so far ran under a real distro's own systemd, which pre-delegates `memory`/`pids`/`cpu`/`cpuset` to its own hierarchy by default -- masking this gap completely until 192.168.15.95 (`kanxeod` as real PID 1, no systemd) hit it live. The project already had the right pattern in place for `io`/`cpuset` specifically (`cgroup_enable_io_accounting()`/`cgroup_enable_cpuset()`, called once at daemon startup) but had never extended it to `memory`/`pids`/`cpu`.

Diagnosed via local-vs-remote reproduction: `--memory-max=`/`--pids-max=` worked perfectly on this dev sandbox with the identical binary, ruling out a code logic bug before the cgroup-delegation read-through even started.

**Fix**: the two existing functions consolidated into one, `cgroup_enable_controllers(void)` (`src/cgroup.c`), writing `"+io +cpuset +memory +pids +cpu"` to the root's `cgroup.subtree_control` in a single call -- cgroup v2 accepts multiple space-separated `+controller` tokens in one write. Clean cut-over (no third function kept alongside the old two, per this project's own no-backward-compat-shims convention); `include/container.h`'s declaration and every doc-comment cross-reference (`daemon/src/main.c`, `daemon/include/swap.h`, `src/overlay.c`) updated to match. Same best-effort, never-fatal posture the io/cpuset handling already established, just extended to cover memory/pids/cpu.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); full local regression sweep, including the tests that actually exercise resource-limited container creation (`test_container_lifecycle`, `test_container_stats`, `test_devices`, `test_container_net`), all passing.

**Still open, explicitly not this fix's scope**: `registry_create()`/`create_container_from_body()` still collapse any `container_create()` failure into a generic, undiagnosed 500 -- the silent-failure gap that made this regression hard to trace from the API alone (local reproduction was required). A real fix belongs to the same "surface real diagnostics" precedent already applied to `pkg.c`'s build-container-prep path, and remains tracked as follow-on work. Deploying this fix to 192.168.15.95 and live-verifying `--memory-max=`/`--pids-max=`/`--network=` containers succeed, plus closing the long-pending lldap/LDAPS tasks (#623/#624) this was blocking, are the immediate next steps.

## Part 25 (done): container creation/exit diagnostics visibility (ADR-0080)

Deploying Part 24's fix to 192.168.15.95 and rebooting surfaced a *new*, more severe symptom: every container after the first one created post-reboot failed to exec (regardless of image, command, or flags), with nothing beyond a bare numeric `exit_status` (e.g. `142`) to explain why. Direct user instruction at this point: don't keep digging blind on the live box -- first close the diagnostics visibility gap that made both this and the still-open "mkbootroot exited 1" bug (Part 23) hard to root-cause, redeploy with it, then continue investigating with real data.

Root cause of the *visibility* gap: `src/container.c`'s child process already had a rich, deliberately-designed numeric exit-code encoding (110-119 named pre-exec setup stages, 130-136 `overlay_create()`'s six named steps, 141-255 a real mount(2)/exec errno sharing one range) -- but the only place any of it was ever explained in human terms was a `perror()` writing to this process's own stdout/stderr, which reaches nobody on a real installed box (`kanxeod` as PID 1, no console, no journal). The shared 141-255 range is also genuinely ambiguous by design (overlay mount(2) vs. the final `execve()` are mutually exclusive within one run, but indistinguishable from the bare number alone).

**Fix**: `container_create()` now opens an always-on (not opt-in, unlike the existing `capture_output` build-log feature) `O_CLOEXEC` diagnostic pipe before `clone3()`; every pre-exec/exec failure path writes a real `"step: strerror(errno)"` line to it via a new `child_diag()` helper. `container_read_diag()` reads it back (never blocks -- the writer's fd is always already closed by the time a caller gets here, either via CLOEXEC on a successful exec or via the child's own `_exit()`); `container_decode_exit_status()` provides a fixed-category fallback for the rarer case it's empty. `registry_entry` gains `last_exit_reason`, surfaced as a new `exit_reason` field on every container JSON response and logged to `GET /system/logs` on any non-zero exit. `create_container_from_body()`'s own generic "failed to create container" 500 (the same gap ADR-0079 already flagged for the cgroup case) now carries the real `strerror(errno)` too. `spawn_kanxeo_bootroot_assembly()` gets the identical treatment for `mkbootroot`'s own stdout/stderr -- direct progress toward finally root-causing Part 23's own still-open assembly failure.

Verified locally: a container given a genuinely missing binary now reports `exit_reason: "child: execve(/usr/bin/does-not-exist): No such file or directory"` instead of a bare `exit_status: 142`. Also ran 5 sequential local container creations with the exact `run` shape that failed on 192.168.15.95 -- all succeeded cleanly, confirming that box's own "every container after the first fails" symptom is environment-specific to it, not a general code bug; this diagnostics work is the tooling needed to root-cause it there next, not a fix for it itself.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); full local regression sweep passing. Redeploying to 192.168.15.95 and continuing the live investigation with the new visibility is the immediate next step.

## Part 26 (done): cgroup atomic-write regression fix (ADR-0081)

Redeploying Part 25's diagnostics and testing live against `kanxeo-builder` (a real image with genuine coreutils, unlike the empty `base` image the earlier confusion involved) surfaced the real root cause immediately: `--memory-max=`, `--pids-max=`, and `--cpuset=` all failed identically with `cgroup_create: No such file or directory` -- including `--cpuset=`, which had worked fine under the pre-ADR-0079 code just hours earlier in this same investigation.

Root cause: ADR-0079's own `cgroup_enable_controllers()` wrote all five wanted controllers (`io`/`cpuset`/`memory`/`pids`/`cpu`) in one combined `cgroup.subtree_control` write. That write is atomic across every token it contains -- if even one of the five is unavailable on the running kernel, the kernel rejects the whole write, silently regressing `io`/`cpuset` back to undelegated alongside `memory`/`pids`/`cpu` never actually getting fixed. Fixed by reading `cgroup.controllers` first and only requesting tokens genuinely listed there, in `src/cgroup.c` (ADR-0081) -- guarantees every actually-available controller gets delegated regardless of what any given kernel happens to lack.

Also resolved as a byproduct of Part 25's own diagnostics: the earlier-suspected "every container after the first one fails to exec" regression on 192.168.15.95 was never a real code regression at all -- `base` is a genuinely empty, zero-package image, and every container run against it (the very first one included, never actually verified before assuming success) failed identically on a missing `/usr/bin/sleep`. Confirmed by rerunning against `kanxeo-builder`, a real image with installed coreutils, where flagless containers succeed cleanly and the resource-limit flags now succeed too with this fix.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); full local regression sweep passing.

## Part 27 (done): kernel SMP + virtio-balloon (ADR-0082)

User-reported: Proxmox's own view of 192.168.15.95's memory kept climbing while idle. Traced to a missing `CONFIG_VIRTIO_BALLOON` -- guest-internal accounting (`GET /v1/system/stats`) stayed flat at ~62MB "used" across repeated polls, so there was never a real leak; a KVM guest with no balloon driver simply can't hand physical pages back to the host once touched, so QEMU's own reported RSS for the VM can only grow.

While separately chasing the still-open `--cpuset=` regression (ADR-0081's fix didn't resolve it), found the real cause: `init/Kconfig`'s own `config CPUSETS` has `depends on SMP`, and `CONFIG_SMP` was never present anywhere in `image/kernel/qemu-part1.config` -- every kernel this project has ever built has been running strictly uniprocessor, on every deployment, regardless of vCPU count. Not just the cpuset bug's real cause -- silently wasted capacity on every workload this project has ever run.

Fixed by adding `CONFIG_SMP=y` and `CONFIG_VIRTIO_BALLOON=y` to `image/kernel/qemu-part1.config`, rebuilt from a clean `allnoconfig` (not layered onto the stale non-SMP `.config`, since SMP makes many previously-hidden Kconfig symbols reachable). Deployed to 192.168.15.95 (direct squashfs+kernel push, `update --image= --kernel=` + `reboot`, landing on slot A) and live-verified: `--cpuset=0` and `--cpuset=1` container creation both succeed post-reboot, confirming a genuine second CPU is active, not just cpuset syntax being accepted. Memory-ballooning's own effect on Proxmox's reported VM RSS still needs a hypervisor-side confirmation (outside this repo's own scope, per this ADR's own Consequences note) -- the guest-side precondition is now satisfied either way.

## Part 28 (done): the real mkbootroot fix -- second ld-linux copy (ADR-0083)

With Part 25's own mkbootroot output-capture diagnostics now deployed, triggered a real `kanxeo` hostbuild round on 192.168.15.95 and read `GET /system/logs` for the actual failure text at last: `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2: No such file or directory`. Root cause: `test_image_fixture_build()` (the control-plane root's own runtime-lib staging function `mkbootroot.c` uses) only ever staged `ld-linux-x86-64.so.2` at `lib64/` -- never the second copy at `lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/` that glibc >= 2.34's own `libc.so.6` needs for its own `DT_NEEDED` on `ld-linux-x86-64.so.2` -- the exact same gap ADR-0057 already fixed for `pkg_seed_image_baseline()` (container images), just never applied to this sibling function serving the control-plane root instead.

Fixed by adding the identical second-copy staging to `test_image_fixture_build()`. Confirmed locally: a fresh `mkbootroot` run now produces both copies in the staged control-plane root. This is the real, final root cause of Part 23's own long-open "kanxeo bootroot assembly: mkbootroot exited 1" failure -- not a workaround, and only findable once ADR-0080's own diagnostics existed to actually read what `mkbootroot` was saying.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); full local regression sweep passing.

**Not the whole story**: deploying this fix and re-triggering a hostbuild+assembly round did not resolve the live symptom -- see Part 29.

## Part 29 (done locally, not yet live-verified): mkbootroot's mksquashfs was unreachable on a real host (ADR-0084)

Re-reading `image/src/mkbootroot.c` in full after Part 28's own fix didn't clear the live symptom surfaced a second, independent bug in the same function: `run_mksquashfs()` execs a hardcoded `/usr/bin/mksquashfs`, real on this dev sandbox but never one of `shelled_bins[]`'s staged binaries -- confirmed absent on any real installed host's own root, since `spawn_kanxeo_bootroot_assembly()` runs `mkbootroot` directly on the daemon's own process with no chroot, meaning its root *is* whatever control-plane squashfs is currently deployed. `squashfs-tools.recipe`'s own header comment claimed this binary was "already staged" -- confirmed false by inspection, an unverified assumption dating to when that recipe was written (ADR-0078).

Fixed by resolving the real exec target dynamically in `mkbootroot.c`'s `main()`: `<host_tools_dir>/usr/bin/mksquashfs` (a real binary `squashfs-tools.recipe` already builds into `kanxeo-hosttools`, alongside `unsquashfs`) when `host_tools_dir` was built, falling back to the dev-sandbox path otherwise -- the exact same tolerant-default shape `host_tool_bins[]` already established for `cp`/`rm`/`sha256sum`/`gzip`, no new mechanism. Also added a `stat()`-based precheck before `execve()` so a future failure of this kind reports unambiguously ("mksquashfs binary not found at `<path>`") instead of needing another investigation to work backward from bare ENOENT text.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); `test_mkbootroot_firmware` passes locally.

**Still not the whole story**: deploying this fix and re-triggering a self-hosted build produced the identical bare `ld-linux-x86-64.so.2: No such file or directory` text again -- see Part 30.

## Part 30 (done, live-verified end-to-end): the real, final mkbootroot root cause -- source paths assumed a merged-usr host (ADR-0085)

Reproduced precisely this time via `strace -f` against a genuinely non-merged-usr chroot built to match 192.168.15.95's own real root exactly (no rich dev-sandbox `/usr`, `host_tools_dir` empty, same real invocation `spawn_kanxeo_bootroot_assembly()` makes). The failing syscall: `test_image_fixture_build()`'s own `openat(AT_FDCWD, "/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2", ...) = -1 ENOENT`, immediately followed by writing that bare text to stderr -- not the dynamic linker, not `mksquashfs`. All three of that function's source reads (lib64/ld-linux, libc.so.6, and ADR-0083's own newly-added lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/ld-linux copy) used `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/...`, which resolves fine on this dev sandbox (`/lib` is itself a symlink to `usr/lib`) but doesn't exist at all on this project's own deliberately non-merged-usr produced roots -- exactly what `mkbootroot` runs on during a self-hosted build. ADR-0083's own fix got the *destination* right and the *source* wrong.

Continuing the same strace-driven verification loop past this fix -- rather than declaring victory on the first clean-looking rebuild -- surfaced two further real gaps of the identical class in the same file: `rm`'s own dev-host fallback path (`/usr/bin/rm`, corrected to `/bin/rm` to match its own deliberately different `bin/rm` destination, unlike its `cp`/`sha256sum`/`gzip` siblings) and a missing `libgcc_s.so.1` (`libpthread`'s own lazy `dlopen()` for `pthread_exit()`'s stack unwinding -- never a `DT_NEEDED` entry, so invisible to every prior `ldd`-based dependency-closure pass this file's own comments already relied on). `mksquashfs` now genuinely starts (ADR-0084) but was aborting at its own ordinary exit.

Fixed all three; verified the *complete* real pipeline end-to-end in the same chroot -- not just a clean compile -- confirming a real, well-formed squashfs is produced (`unsquashfs -l` checked for every expected file) with exit 0, no remaining failures of any kind.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); `test_mkbootroot_firmware` passes locally.

**Live-verified on 192.168.15.95 itself**: deployed (direct squashfs+kernel push, landing on slot B), then re-triggered a genuine self-hosted `pkg hostbuild kanxeo` round from a fresh source push. The assembly's own captured output confirmed both fixes work for real -- no more `ld-linux` failure, no more `rm` failure. The *only* remaining failure was `run_mksquashfs()`'s own new `stat()` diagnostic (ADR-0084) correctly reporting `mksquashfs binary not found at /usr/bin/mksquashfs` -- expected, correct behavior: this box has never had a `kanxeo-hosttools` image built (`image ls` confirmed), so `host_tools_dir` is genuinely empty and the dev-sandbox fallback path is genuinely absent on a real installed root. Not a code bug; a real, separate operational gap -- see Part 32 for closing it out.

This is very likely the real, complete close of the long-open Part 23 "mkbootroot exited 1" failure -- the first time this exact sequence has been verified against a faithful reproduction of the real host's own constraints AND against the real host itself. Part 27's own CONFIG_SMP/CONFIG_VIRTIO_BALLOON kernel is deployed and live-verified separately (`--cpuset=0` and `--cpuset=1` container creation both confirmed working on 192.168.15.95).

## Part 31 (done): stopping __pkgbuild via POST .../stop left pkg.c's job lock stuck forever (ADR-0086)

Found live while building the `kanxeo-hosttools` image (Part 32) needed to close Part 30 out completely: a `perl` build genuinely stalled (`cpu.usage_usec` frozen identically across repeated `kanxeoctl stats` calls 30+ seconds apart, host `load1`/`load5`/`load15` correspondingly near zero). `POST /v1/containers/__pkgbuild/stop` killed it correctly at the container level, but every subsequent `pkg install` then 409'd "another package install is already in progress" indefinitely, with no REST-visible way to recover short of restarting `kanxeod` itself.

Root cause: `handle_stop()` (`daemon/src/main.c`) kills and reaps directly via `registry_remove()`, deliberately bypassing the epoll-driven `handle_container_event()` path (the pidfd is explicitly pulled from epoll first, so this synchronous stop can't race a live event for the same fd) -- but `handle_container_event()` is the *only* place `pkg_build_completed()` (`daemon/src/pkg.c`) normally runs, and therefore the only place `pkg.c`'s own job-lock state (`g_current_job_name` and friends) ever gets cleared. `__pkgbuild`, the reserved build-container name, had never been manually stopped before this session -- the gap had simply never been exercised.

Fixed by having `handle_stop()` call `pkg_build_completed()` itself when the stopped name is `PKG_BUILD_CONTAINER_NAME`, using the real `exit_status` `registry_remove()`'s own `registry_mark_exited()` call already wrote onto the entry -- no new mechanism, the exact same completion path the organic-exit case already uses. A manually-killed build's `exit_status` is never `0`, so the function's own success-only dependency-chaining logic correctly never fires from this path.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); `test_container_lifecycle`/`test_pkg` both pass locally. Deployment to 192.168.15.95 (to actually clear the currently-stuck lock and unblock the host-tools build) is the immediate next step.

## Part 33 (done, live-verified end-to-end): build-output capture pipes deadlocked on sufficiently verbose builds (ADR-0087)

Continuing Part 32's own `kanxeo-hosttools` bootstrap: `perl` (real upstream CPAN URL, real `make -j"$(nproc)"`) reproducibly stalled partway through its own build -- `__pkgbuild`'s `cpu.usage_usec` frozen for 90+ seconds, host `load1`/`load5`/`load15` near zero. An interactive-prompt hang, a DNS-resolution hang, and a parallel-`make` race were each investigated and ruled out with direct evidence. PSI (`cpu.pressure`/`memory.pressure`/`io.pressure`) reading exactly zero, combined with zero disk I/O and a frozen `cpu.usage_usec`, pointed at an in-memory synchronization block rather than any kernel-tracked resource contention.

Root cause, confirmed by reading `daemon/src/pkg.c` directly: the build container's own stdout/stderr capture pipe (a plain `pipe()`, 64KB kernel buffer) was never drained while the container ran -- only once, in a blocking loop, inside `pkg_build_completed()`, called only after the container had already exited. Any build whose combined output exceeded 64KB deadlocked unconditionally, since nothing read from the pipe until that now-unreachable point. `daemon/src/main.c`'s `spawn_kanxeo_bootroot_assembly()`/`handle_bootroot_assemble_event()` turned out to have the identical architectural gap (mkbootroot's own captured stdout/stderr, `cc->output_fd`, read once at reap time) -- not yet observed live, but a real, confirmed-by-inspection latent deadlock in the exact self-hosted assembly step the user's own standing requirement depends on. Fixed pre-emptively in the same pass.

Both pipes are now drained incrementally via the daemon's own epoll loop, modeled directly on `CONN_KMSG`'s existing direct-fd pattern (`start_kmsg_watch()`/`handle_kmsg_event()`) rather than the pidfd-then-single-read shape every other `CONN_*` kind uses: new `CONN_PKG_BUILD_OUTPUT` and `CONN_BOOTROOT_OUTPUT` conn kinds register each pipe's read end (opened `O_NONBLOCK` via `pipe2()`) directly with epoll the moment the corresponding subprocess spawns, draining everything available into a persistent, bounded sliding-window capture buffer on every `EPOLLIN`. The exit-reaper functions (`pkg_build_completed()`, `handle_bootroot_assemble_event()`) now just read from that already-filled buffer, plus one final non-blocking top-off to catch anything written in the brief window before the subprocess's own exit. Each pipe's own close/cleanup is owned exclusively by its own EPOLLIN-driven handler on EOF, not by the exit reaper, avoiding a race between two independently-delivered epoll events for the same fd.

No pipe-size tuning (`F_SETPIPE_SZ`) was considered -- a larger buffer only raises the threshold, it doesn't remove the deadlock for an arbitrarily verbose build, which this project's own "No Stop-Gaps" maxim rules out as a real fix.

Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`, zero warnings); full local regression sweep (`test_pkg`, `test_container_lifecycle`, `test_system_update`, and every other daemon-linked test, all passing). Deployed to 192.168.15.95 via a direct squashfs push to the inactive slot (first freeing the already-wedged `__pkgbuild` job lock via `POST .../stop`, ADR-0086), rebooted into it, then re-ran the real `perl.recipe` (upstream CPAN URL, real `make -j"$(nproc)"`) end to end: `cpu.usage_usec` climbed continuously the entire build with no freeze at the ~90s mark that reliably killed every prior attempt, and `perl` reached `state: "installed"` with its full real file manifest. Deadlock genuinely confirmed fixed, not just theoretically addressed.

## Part 34 (done): kanxeo-hosttools fully built on 192.168.15.95 -- two more real recipe bugs found and fixed

With the pipe deadlock (Part 33) closed, continued Part 32's own `kanxeo-hosttools` build to completion: `perl`, `zlib`, `xz`, `tar`, `gzip`, `bzip2`, `e2fsprogs` installed cleanly; `openssl`, `curl`, and `squashfs-tools` each hit a real, distinct bug along the way, all found and fixed live rather than worked around blindly.

**`openssl.recipe`**: `./Configure`'s own shebang is `#!/usr/bin/env perl`, which needs `/usr/bin/env` on `PATH` -- real on an image that also has `coreutils`, but `kanxeo-hosttools` deliberately doesn't (it's a lean, purpose-built set, not a general toolchain image). Execve() on the un-runnable script fell back to bash interpreting `Configure`'s own Perl source as shell (`"use: command not found"`). Fixed by invoking `perl ./Configure ...` directly in `pkg_build()` -- `pkg_depends="perl"` already guarantees a real `perl` on `PATH`, so this needs nothing new.

**`squashfs-tools.recipe`**: `pkg_build()` and `pkg_install()` run as two calls in the *same* shell session (`pkg.c`'s own build invocation: `. recipe.sh; cd /build/src && pkg_build && pkg_install`) -- `pkg_build()`'s own `cd squashfs-tools` was still in effect when `pkg_install()` ran, so its own `cp -a squashfs-tools/mksquashfs ...` was looking one directory too deep (`cannot stat 'squashfs-tools/mksquashfs'`). A real bug in a previously-"verified" recipe, evidently never actually build-tested end to end before this session. Fixed by dropping the redundant `squashfs-tools/` prefix in `pkg_install()`.

**A separate, still only partially understood issue, worked around rather than root-caused**: `kanxeod`'s own fixed host-side `curl` (`PKG_CURL_BIN`) failed fetching two different `pkg_source` URLs -- `openssl-3.0.20.tar.gz`'s real release asset (both via `openssl.org`'s own redirecting `/source/` URL and the direct GitHub URL it 301s to) and, separately, `squashfs-tools`' own GitHub tag-archive URL -- both with a bare `curl exit 1` ("unsupported protocol"). Not a blanket HTTPS/DNS/CDN problem: a structurally-identical GitHub release-asset redirect for a different package (`xz-5.8.3.tar.gz`) succeeded from the same box in the same session, and this dev sandbox's own `curl` fetches every one of these same URLs without issue. No shell access on the real box to run `curl -v` and see the actual failure directly, so the true cause is still open. Unblocked live, both times, with the established LAN-serve trick (`docs/guides/remote-development.md`): fetch the file here, checksum-verify it byte-identical to the recipe's own `pkg_sha256`, serve it from this sandbox's own IP, temporarily repoint the *live, already-registered* recipe at that LAN URL just long enough to install, then push the real recipe (unmodified, canonical URL) straight back once done -- the box's own persisted recipe registry now matches the committed files exactly, nothing LAN-specific left behind. Documented as a standing environment note in `CLAUDE.md` for the next time this is hit.

Full clean rebuild + regression sweep (unchanged from Part 33, no new C code this part -- pure recipe fixes); live-verified end to end: all 10 `kanxeo-hosttools` packages (`perl`, `openssl`, `zlib`, `curl`, `tar`, `bzip2`, `xz`, `squashfs-tools`, `e2fsprogs`, `gzip`) show `state: "installed"` with real file manifests on 192.168.15.95.

## Part 35 (done, live-verified end-to-end): CONFIG_VETH was never enabled -- every real container network attachment has always failed (ADR-0088)

Immediately hit while starting the actual DNS-container work this session was building toward: `POST /v1/containers` with any `networks` attachment on 192.168.15.95 failed with a bare `"Operation not supported"` (500), for both a network with a real physical uplink (`management`) and a pure isolated bridge (`chicken`) -- ruling out anything IP- or upstream-specific. Traced to `src/container_net.c`'s `rtnl_veth_create()`, the sole mechanism any container has ever gotten a network interface through, failing its `RTM_NEWLINK` call with `ENOTSUP`/`EOPNOTSUPP` -- the kernel's own answer when no driver is registered for the requested link kind. A full `git log -p` grep confirmed `CONFIG_VETH` has never once appeared in `image/kernel/qemu-part1.config`'s history -- `CONFIG_BRIDGE=y` (enabled since early in the project) is necessary but not sufficient, since a bridge on its own has nothing to attach without either veth or a real NIC.

This had gone uncaught through every prior "container networking works" milestone (Phase 24's VRRP/OSPF router pairs, the console/exec work, disk/PKI/DNS testing) because all of it ran on this dev sandbox's own host kernel, which has `CONFIG_VETH` natively like any ordinary distro kernel -- never on a kernel actually built from this project's own tracked config fragment. Fixed by adding `CONFIG_VETH=y` immediately after the existing `CONFIG_BRIDGE=y`/`CONFIG_VLAN_8021Q=y` block.

Kernel rebuilt from the cached `allnoconfig` source tree already on hand from the earlier CONFIG_SMP fix (ADR-0082). Deployed as a kernel-only change: the control-plane root squashfs was rebuilt locally via `build/mkbootroot` to exactly match what was already running on the box (no `kanxeod` code changes bundled in), and both files were pushed together onto 192.168.15.95's inactive slot A -- per the documented one-sided-update footgun (task #671) -- via the established LAN-serve + throwaway-scratch-recipe fetch trick (`docs/guides/remote-development.md`), then a real reboot.

Live-verified post-reboot: `GET /system/boot` confirmed `slot: "a"` (the newly-written slot actually booted, not a silent fallback), then a real container (`vethtest`, image `base`, attached to `management`) was created successfully and received a real veth-backed interface with its assigned IP (`192.168.15.1`) -- the identical request that previously failed outright. See ADR-0088 for the full writeup, including the still-open gap that no automated test in this project's suite exercises a real kernel built from `qemu-part1.config` attaching a container to a network, so a future regression of this exact kind still wouldn't be caught by CI.

## Part 36 (done, live-verified end-to-end): dns-1/dns-2 stood up; root-netns ip_forward was never enabled either (ADR-0089); container-create log use-after-free fixed (ADR-0090)

The originally-requested task -- two real `dnsmasq` containers (`dns-1`/`192.168.15.101`, `dns-2`/`192.168.15.102`) on the `management` network, registered as DNS servers, host resolver repointed at both -- ran straight into a second real, previously-undiscovered networking gap immediately after Part 35's own CONFIG_VETH fix: internal resolution worked fine (dnsmasq's own local records, no forwarding needed) but real recursive resolution against the configured upstream forwarders (`1.1.1.1`/`8.8.8.8`) timed out unconditionally.

Isolated with controlled before/after tests against a throwaway container on the same bridge: reaching the daemon's own management address worked, reaching one hop further (the real upstream LAN gateway) did not, and DNS recursion itself -- the exact functional path in question -- was the clean, decisive before/after signal once the actual fix landed. Root cause: `net.ipv4.ip_forward` was never enabled in the daemon's own root netns anywhere in this codebase. The existing `--ip-forward` container-create flag (`container_net_enable_ip_forward()`, `src/container_net.c`) only ever writes this sysctl inside a **container's own** netns -- built for a container acting as its own router between two of its own attached networks (Phase 24's BIRD/keepalived pairs), never for the **host's** root netns that every ordinary (non-router) container's default route already points at, by ADR-0067's own explicit design (a network's own "address" is deliberately the gateway its containers route through). Never caught before because no container prior to `dns-1`/`dns-2` had ever needed to originate a connection leaving the host at all -- every earlier "container needs the internet" case (package installs) was always a `kanxeod`-side `curl`, never container-initiated.

Separately, while investigating this the user had already flagged (a prior segment) garbled container names in the container-create-failure log line, visible in the web dashboard -- root-caused as a real use-after-free (`daemon/src/main.c`'s `REGISTRY_ERR_CREATE_FAILED` branch logging a `json_as_string()` pointer into the request body's own JSON tree *after* that tree had already been `json_free()`'d). Fixed in the same pass and shipped in the same deploy.

#### Fixed
- `daemon/src/main.c`: enable `net.ipv4.ip_forward=1` in the root netns once at daemon startup, right after `network_init()` -- fatal on failure, matching this codebase's existing fail-fast posture for core network-capability setup.
- `daemon/src/main.c`: container-create's failed-creation log line now copies the container name into a stack buffer before the JSON tree is freed, instead of using the dangling pointer directly.

Deployed as a root-squashfs-only update (kernel unchanged from Part 35, resupplied anyway per the one-sided-update footgun already documented, task #671) via the same local `build/mkbootroot` + LAN-serve + scratch-recipe mechanism, then a real reboot.

Live-verified end to end: `dns-1`/`dns-2` both resolve `kanxeo.uk.home.arpa` (internal) and `example.com` (real, recursive, via the configured upstream forwarders) correctly and independently; both registered as DNS servers (`GET /v1/dns/servers`); the host's own resolver (`GET /v1/system/resolv`) repointed at both, replacing the earlier temporary placeholder addresses from an earlier segment. See ADR-0089 and ADR-0090 for the full writeups.

## Part 37 (done, live-verified end-to-end): real LDAPS handshake + LDAP bind against lldap, closing tasks #623/#624

The lldap deployment left open since the multi-source-recipe phase several parts back: `ldapsvc` (image `lldap`, `management` network, `192.168.15.105`) run with its LDAPS-enabled config staged via `--file=`, a PKI-issued cert (`--pki-issue --pki-cert-dir=/opt/lldap`), `--restart=always`. Needed a real `bash -c "cd /opt/lldap && exec ./lldap run ..."` wrapper -- lldap's own `app/` web assets resolve relative to its process's CWD, no config option of its own controls it, and container creation has no "workdir" flag; `bash` installed onto the `lldap` image for exactly this.

Verified in two independent layers, not just one: `openssl s_client` confirmed the PKI chain itself (`subject=CN = ldapsvc`, `issuer=CN = Kanxeo Root CA`, TLS 1.3, `Verify return code: 0 (ok)`); a real `ldapwhoami` simple bind, hostname-verified through a real DNS record resolved via `dns-1` (not skipping hostname checks), confirmed the LDAP protocol layer itself (`Result: Success (0)`, `dn:uid=admin,ou=people,dc=kanxeo,dc=local`).

Found and fixed a real git-hygiene drift along the way: 192.168.15.95's own persisted `bash` recipe still pointed at a stale, no-longer-served LAN URL from an earlier session's diagnostic workaround -- repointed back to the canonical `ftp.gnu.org` source to match the committed file exactly. Also confirmed the `curl exit 1` ("unsupported protocol") fetch quirk already documented for GitHub release assets isn't GitHub-specific -- reproduced identically for `ftp.gnu.org`'s own bash tarball, unblocked the same way via the LAN-serve trick. No code changes this part.

## Part 38 (done, live-verified end-to-end across a real reboot): DNS server bindings were never persisted; auto-registered records were never site-qualified (ADR-0091, ADR-0092)

Two real, previously-undiscovered bugs, both raised directly by the user testing the freshly-deployed DNS work rather than found proactively: `dns server ls` came back empty despite `dns-1`/`dns-2` having been registered earlier the same session, `ldapsvc.uk.home.arpa` failed to resolve while `dns-1`/`dns-2`/`kanxeo.uk.home.arpa` all worked, and a direct question -- why did `dns-1`/`dns-2` have no site domain suffix, unlike every manually-created record?

Root cause 1: `dns_server_register()`'s own bindings were purely in-memory (`g_bindings[]`, `daemon/src/dns.c`), never persisted, unlike the DNS record set itself -- silently lost on the very next daemon restart/reboot, explaining both the empty server list and why a record created after that reboot (`ldapsvc`) never reached either server's own hosts file (nothing was left registered to sync it to). Root cause 2: the container `--dns-register` auto-registration path called `dns_record_create()` directly, skipping the `siteconfig_qualify()` (ADR-0052) call the manual `POST /v1/dns/records` path has always made -- a real inconsistency, not a design choice.

Fixed: `dns_init()` gained a second persisted state file for server bindings, loaded at startup and explicitly re-synced once boot-time container autostart completes (bindings loaded before any container has actually started have nothing live to push to yet); the auto-registration path now qualifies names the same way the manual path does; `dns_record_forget_owner()` (container-delete cleanup) was fixed alongside it to search by `owner_container` rather than assume name == container_name, a assumption the qualification fix would otherwise have silently broken, leaking records on every future container delete.

Live-verified across a real reboot, not just in-process: registered `dns-1`/`dns-2` again post-deploy, rebooted a second time with no further manual action, confirmed both the server registrations and real resolution (`ldapsvc.uk.home.arpa` via both servers) survived intact. Full local `test_dns` regression sweep clean before deploy. See ADR-0091 and ADR-0092 for the full writeups.

## Part 39 (done, local regression sweep clean; not yet deployed live): git-archive tarball extraction, and hostbuild's own permanent-409 gap (ADR-0093, ADR-0094)

Two backlog items closed together, both in `daemon/src/pkg.c`. `extract_tarball()` always ran `tar --strip-components=1` unconditionally, assuming every source tarball has a real release's own wrapping directory -- true for every upstream recipe's tarball in this project, false for `kanxeo.recipe`'s own self-build source snapshot (a `git archive`-generated tarball from gitea's REST API, which without an explicit `--prefix=` has no wrapping directory at all, meaning the real top-level tree sits at depth 0). Blindly stripping one component there silently drops or misplaces real content. Fixed by inspecting the tarball's own listing first and only stripping when every entry genuinely shares one common top-level component.

Separately, `pkg_hostbuild_start()` made every hostbuild request for an already-`PKG_STATE_INSTALLED` name a bare, permanent 409 -- no way to rebuild from fresh source under an unchanged recipe name. Given the same `upgrade` parameter `pkg_install_start()` already has (`POST /pkg/hostbuild {"upgrade": true}`, `kanxeoctl pkg hostbuild ... --upgrade`), with identical semantics: still 409 if the recipe's own version hasn't actually changed.

Full local regression sweep clean (`test_pkg`, `test_dns`, `test_daemon`, `test_cli`), zero compiler warnings. Not yet deployed to 192.168.15.95 or exercised against a real git-archive tarball live -- both are code-correctness fixes reasoned directly from the documented failure mode, not re-derived from a fresh live reproduction this pass. See ADR-0093 and ADR-0094.

## Part 40 (done, live-verified via a real QEMU boot-update round trip): the /system/update one-sided image_path/kernel_path footgun, closed at the source (ADR-0095)

`POST /system/update`'s `image_path`/`kernel_path` have always been independently optional, but omitting one used to leave the inactive slot's own copy of that file exactly as stale as it was from whenever that slot was last written -- a real footgun documented and manually worked around (always resupply both, even the unchanged one) in this project's own guides since early on, never fixed at the source until now (task #671).

Fixed: `do_system_update()` now auto-fills whichever half is omitted from the *active* slot's own currently-running copy (already booted, already known-good) rather than leaving the inactive slot's prior content stale. Both are always written on every successful call now; the response's own `updated` field simplifies to always `["root", "kernel"]`. Explicitly supplying both is completely unaffected -- verified via the existing QEMU-based `test_boot_update` (real device/ESP writes, a real second power-on confirming the freshly-written slot boots), still clean. The new auto-fill path itself was verified by code review and the local `test_system_update` sweep, not a new dedicated QEMU scenario -- acknowledged as a real gap, not glossed over. `docs/guides/remote-development.md` and `docs/guides/kernel-build-and-ab-updates.md` updated to drop the now-unnecessary operator discipline. See ADR-0095.

## Part 41 (done, local regression sweep clean): pkg fetch failures now capture curl's own real error text, closed at the root rather than worked around (ADR-0096)

Found live, mid-redeploy of `kanxeo` v1.7.0 to 192.168.15.95: a `pkg hostbuild` fetch failed with only `"fetch failed (curl exit status 1)"` -- `start_fetch_for()`'s curl child never captured its own stderr anywhere, so there was no way to tell *why*. Reaching for the established LAN-serve deploy workaround (real, but built for a different problem -- a fresh install with no SSH/shell) was explicitly rejected as the wrong move: it would have unblocked the deploy without ever explaining the failure. Fixed at the source instead.

Fixed: the curl child now pipes its own stderr to a small sidecar file (`fetch_error_sidecar_path()`) via a short-lived `pipe2()`/`dup2()` on failure -- a single synchronous `read()` after `waitpid()` is sufficient (curl has already exited, unlike the build path's still-running output, so there's no deadlock risk to design around and no need for ADR-0087's epoll-drained pipe machinery). `pkg_fetch_completed()` reads it back, folds curl's own real error text into `e->error`, and logs it via `logstore_write()` for the first time. Full local regression sweep clean (`test_pkg`, `test_daemon`, `test_cli`, `test_dns`, `test_system_update`), zero compiler warnings. No new dedicated fetch-failure test this pass -- reproducing a real curl stderr failure needs a fake failing HTTP fixture the sandboxed test harness doesn't have; a real, acknowledged gap. See ADR-0096.

## Part 42 (done, live-verified via a real kanxeo v1.7.1 hostbuild): mkbootroot never staged a CA certificate bundle (ADR-0097)

Found immediately after Part 41's own fetch-diagnostic capture landed, on the very next redeploy attempt: with a separate, real, live-only DNS-forwarding gap fixed too (`dns-1`/`dns-2` never forwarding the operator's own `home.arpa` zone upstream to their real home-network resolver -- fixed directly on the box, not a code change, once the operator supplied the real resolver IPs), the same fetch failed a second, different way -- `curl: (77) error setting certificate file: /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt`. `curl.recipe`'s own build-time auto-detected default CA path has never actually been staged onto the assembled control-plane root by `mkbootroot.c` -- `openssl.cnf` gets exactly this treatment already, the CA bundle never did.

Fixed: `mkbootroot.c` now stages a real `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt` immediately after the existing `openssl.cnf` staging block, same mechanism, sourced from wherever `mkbootroot` itself runs. Live-verified about as strongly as possible: the exact `kanxeo` v1.7.1 hostbuild that surfaced this bug (real HTTPS, token-authenticated, `git.home.arpa`) completed successfully -- fetch, build, and install -- once this fix (and the DNS fix) were deployed. A separate, real gap surfaced immediately after and is intentionally not fixed here: the server-side mkbootroot re-assembly that normally follows a successful `kanxeo` hostbuild (ADR-0057) itself fails with `sha256sum: No such file or directory`, sourcing from the `kanxeo-hosttools` image's own rootfs -- that image was never given a real `sha256sum` binary. Tracked separately (task #735). See ADR-0097.

## Part 43 (done, verified in isolation + local regression sweep): tarball_has_common_top_dir() corrupted its own capture while draining it (ADR-0098)

Found live fixing task #735 (installing `coreutils` onto `kanxeo-hosttools` to provide the missing `sha256sum`): ADR-0093's own 64KB tarball-listing capture cut off mid-prefix for `coreutils-9.11.tar.xz` (a 137KB real listing). Two compounding bugs: the truncated trailing line false-positived a mismatch, and (found only after that first fix alone didn't resolve the live symptom) the existing post-capture drain loop reused the same capture buffer as its own scratch space, overwriting the real captured head with arbitrary tail fragments before the mismatch scan ran.

Fixed: the drain now reads into a separate discard buffer instead of reusing the capture; the trailing partial line is still trimmed off whenever the capture was truncated. Verified in isolation first -- a standalone extraction of the fixed function against the real tarball confirmed correct behavior (`result=1`) before redeploying. Full local regression sweep clean, zero compiler warnings. See ADR-0098.

## Part 44 (done, local regression sweep clean): GET /disks reports real mount status, not diskformat.c's own ephemeral job history (ADR-0099, closes task #672)

`diskformat.c`'s own `DISKFORMAT_STATE_READY`/`NONE` looked like it answered "is this disk mounted" but doesn't -- purely in-memory, per-daemon-process state for the single most recent format job this daemon itself ran, forgotten across every restart even though a real mount persists, and blind to a disk mounted by hand or from before this mechanism existed.

Fixed: `disk_enumerate()` now does one real pass over `/proc/mounts` after building the disk list, matching each mounted device back to its parent whole disk the same way `resolve_os_disk_name()` already does. `GET /disks` gains `mounted`/`mount_path` per entry, live ground truth on every call; `kanxeoctl disks` shows a mounted/not-mounted column. Full local regression sweep clean, zero compiler warnings. No new dedicated test -- `GET /disks` has never had one, real `/sys/class/block` enumeration isn't practical to exercise deterministically in the sandboxed harness; a real, pre-existing gap, not worsened here. See ADR-0099.

## Part 45 (done, local regression sweep clean): container cmd/argv is finally REST-visible after creation (ADR-0100, closes task #675)

A container's own entrypoint was write-only: `POST /v1/containers`'s `"cmd"` field only ever pointed into that request's transient parsed JSON tree, freed immediately after the process was spawned, and nothing durable ever stored it -- there was no REST-visible way to ask a running or stopped container "what are you actually running."

Fixed: `struct registry_entry` (`daemon/include/registry.h`) gains `cmd[]`/`cmd_count`, copied from `spec->argv` in `registry_create()` the same way `interfaces[]`/`sysctls[]` already are; `registry_write_json_one()` emits it as a `"cmd"` array. For genuinely stopped containers, `containerdef.c`'s `write_stopped_def_json_one()` parses `"cmd"` back out of the persisted create-request body it already re-parses for `image` -- no new storage for that case. `include/container.h` gains named `CONTAINER_MAX_ARGV`/`CONTAINER_ARGV_MAX` bounds, replacing a bare `argv_buf[64]` literal. `kanxeoctl`'s container listing and the web dashboard's container Summary tab both surface it. Full local regression sweep clean (`test_daemon`, `test_cli`, `test_container_lifecycle`, `test_container_files`, `test_container_net`, `test_networks`, `test_devices`, `test_container_restart`), zero compiler warnings. See ADR-0100.

## Part 46 (done, local regression sweep clean, real end-to-end test): live-tail a real in-flight package build's own output (ADR-0101, closes task #676)

A build's real-time stdout/stderr had no REST-visible path while still running -- ADR-0087's own epoll-drained capture buffer was only ever read back out on a *failed* build; a successful build's output was simply discarded. The existing container console looked like it might cover this by pointing it at the build container's own name, but doesn't: its output is a plain pipe, not a PTY, and the console's `exec_into_container()` spawns a brand-new process rather than attaching to the running one.

Fixed: a new, purpose-built `GET /v1/pkg/build/log` WebSocket endpoint (`daemon/src/main.c`'s `CONN_PKG_BUILD_LOG_WS`/`try_pkg_build_log_upgrade()`) -- a one-way relay, structurally much simpler than the console (no exec/PTY pairing, `cc` is the entire session). `pkg_build_output_readable()` (`daemon/src/pkg.c`) gained an optional out-parameter for the raw bytes read each call; a new `pkg_build_output_snapshot()` replays the captured-so-far tail to a client attaching mid-build. Up to 4 concurrent viewers; 404 with no build in flight; a real WS close frame (tearing every attached client down) the instant the build finishes. `client/src/console.c` gained `kx_pkg_build_log_run()` (`kanxeoctl pkg build-log`), sharing a generalized `do_ws_handshake()` with the existing console client. New permanent test `test/test_pkg_build_log.c` proves the whole path end to end against a real daemon and a real build container (hand-rolled WS client, same precedent `test_console_exec.c` set): 404/400 negative cases, live incremental marker delivery strictly before build completion, a genuine close frame, and a fresh 404 once torn down. Full local regression sweep clean, zero compiler warnings. Deliberately scoped to the pkg build pipe only -- `CONN_BOOTROOT_OUTPUT`'s structurally identical capture is a natural later follow-on, not done here. See ADR-0101.

## Part 47 (done, local regression sweep clean): per-container disk selection at creation time (ADR-0102, closes task #638, Phase D)

The `"container-storage"` disk role (Phase B, `diskrole.c`) was assignable via `POST /diskroles` but nothing ever read it -- `POST /v1/containers` always placed a container's writable overlay storage under the fixed OS-disk `CONTAINERS_DIR`, with no way to say "put this container's data on disk X." This was the previously-open "Phase D: container-storage migration" `diskrole.h`'s own comment referenced.

Fixed: a new optional `"disk"` field on `POST /v1/containers`. `resolve_container_disk_root()` (`daemon/src/main.c`) validates the named disk exists, is currently mounted (ADR-0099's live ground truth), and carries the `"container-storage"` role before rooting `upper`/`work`/`merged` under `<disk's mount_path>/containers/<name>` instead of `CONTAINERS_DIR/<name>` -- any failure is a specific 400, not generic. `struct registry_entry` gained `disk_name` (echoed as `"disk"` in `GET /containers`); `container_root_for()` re-resolves a disk's current mount_path fresh for the two other call sites that need it (file-read on a stopped container, stats' upperdir-size read), matching ADR-0099's "live state, not a cache" precedent. A `restart:"always"` container's own persisted create-request body already carries `"disk"` through unchanged, so autostart replay places it correctly with no separate persistence mechanism. `kanxeoctl run --disk=NAME` is the CLI surface. Full local regression sweep clean, zero compiler warnings. The web dashboard has no disk picker yet (acknowledged gap); no new automated test for the successful-placement path itself (this sandbox's visible block devices are real host hardware, unsafe to format for a test -- same gap ADR-0099 already accepted). Found but deliberately not fixed here, own separate scope (task #738): `DELETE /v1/containers` has never removed a container's on-disk directories, for any placement. See ADR-0102.

## Part 48 (done, full clean rebuild + full regression sweep, no live-hardware verification): real btrfs qgroup-based disk quotas as a second backend alongside ext4 (ADR-0103, closes task #678)

`--disk-quota=BYTES` (Part 4, ADR-0062) only ever meant an ext4 project quota via `quotactl(2)` -- btrfs has no `quotactl(2)` support at all, and a fundamentally different, subvolume-scoped qgroup model instead, so a btrfs-backed container disk's quota request silently had no real enforcement. Per explicit user direction (2026-08-08): close this gap for real rather than document it, using raw ioctls rather than shelling out to btrfs-progs, matching this project's own convention (rtnetlink instead of `ip`, raw ICMP instead of `ping`).

Fixed: `overlay_backing_is_btrfs()` (`src/overlay.c`, `statfs()` against `KX_BTRFS_SUPER_MAGIC`) is the single branch point both `overlay_create()` (on upperdir's parent, since upperdir doesn't exist yet at check time) and `daemon/src/main.c`'s quota-decision block (on `container_base`) call. On btrfs, `overlay_create_btrfs_upperdir()` creates upperdir as a real subvolume (`BTRFS_IOC_SUBVOL_CREATE`) instead of a plain directory, then -- if a quota was requested -- enables btrfs quotas (`BTRFS_IOC_QUOTA_CTL`) and sets a qgroup hard limit (`BTRFS_IOC_QGROUP_LIMIT`) using btrfs's own `qgroupid=0` self-addressing convention ("the subvolume owning this fd"), eliminating any need for a persisted qgroup-id-tracking table the originating task description had anticipated needing. `struct overlay_spec` gained a `quota_bytes` field, kept parallel to (not overloading) the existing ext4-only `project_id`. All new ioctl structs/numbers hand-transcribed into `include/linux_compat.h` (same convention as `clone3`/`epoll_event`/`fsxattr`), each number cross-checked by independently re-deriving this project's own two already-correct `FS_IOC_FS{GET,SET}XATTR` constants with the identical derivation method. Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`) zero warnings; full regression sweep (21 daemon-linked tests plus `test_harness`/`test_overlay`/`test_container_net`/`test_devices`) clean, confirming the ext4 path is byte-for-byte unaffected. The btrfs success path itself has not been live-tested -- this sandbox has kernel btrfs support but no mounted btrfs filesystem, no `mkfs.btrfs`, and no disk safe to reformat (same acknowledged gap as ADR-0099/ADR-0102); live verification against real hardware remains an open follow-up. Deliberately does not extend `diskformat.c` (still ext4-only) to offer btrfs as a format choice -- re-scoped task #732 (previously a near-duplicate of #678) tracks that separate remaining piece. See ADR-0103.

## Part 49 (done, full clean rebuild + full regression sweep, kernel-image-dependent QEMU boot test not re-run): real btrfs disk formatting alongside ext4 (ADR-0104, closes task #732)

Part 48/ADR-0103 closed the *quota enforcement* half of the ext4-vs-btrfs gap; `POST /v1/disks/{name}/format` was still hardcoded ext4 end to end -- it shells out to a staged `mkfs.ext4` and mounts with `mount(dev, path, "ext4", ...)`, no way to ask for anything else.

Fixed: a new optional `fs_type` field (`"ext4"`/`"btrfs"`, default `"ext4"` -- every pre-existing request keeps its exact prior behavior) on `POST /v1/disks/{name}/format`, validated in `daemon/src/main.c` before `diskformat_start()` is called. `diskformat_start()` (`daemon/src/diskformat.c`) now takes a real `enum diskformat_fs_type` and picks its mkfs binary and `mount(2)` fstype string from it, inside the same existing fork+exec+mount sequence -- not a parallel code path. `mkfs.btrfs` is staged into `image/src/mkbootroot.c`'s assembled boot image tolerantly (`stat()`-gated, reusing the existing `host_tools_dir` argument, no new positional arg) rather than added to the unconditionally-required staging lists mkfs.ext4 itself uses -- this dev sandbox has no `mkfs.btrfs` of its own and no dev-host fallback exists for it, matching firmware/module-staging's own "absent is a normal, silently-skipped state" precedent. Its runtime library closure needed no new entries at all (confirmed via a real local `ldd`: identical to `mke2fs`'s own plus `libz.so.1`, both already staged). New `pkg/recipes/btrfs-progs.recipe` (v7.1, kernel.org's own canonical release, builds only `mkfs.btrfs` matching `e2fsprogs.recipe`'s single-binary economy) and `pkg/recipes/libblkid.recipe` (reuses `libuuid.recipe`'s already-staged libuuid via pkg-config rather than rebuilding it) -- both verified via a real local build in this sandbox. `kanxeoctl disks format NAME --fs-type=btrfs` is the CLI surface. Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`) zero warnings; full regression sweep (21 daemon-linked tests, `test_mkbootroot_firmware`) clean. `test_boot` (QEMU end-to-end) hit a pre-existing, already-documented environment gap (`build/bzImage` not reproduced by `make clean`) unrelated to this change -- the squashfs assembly step this change actually touches completed successfully before that point. The btrfs mkfs/mount success path itself has not been live-tested end to end -- same acknowledged sandbox gap as ADR-0099/ADR-0102/ADR-0103. See ADR-0104.

## Part 50 (done, full clean rebuild + full regression sweep): real freshness tracking for `pkg hostbuild kanxeo --deploy` (ADR-0105, closes task #737)

`kanxeod-root.squashfs` existing at a hostbuild's own `artifact_path` was treated as "ready to deploy" as soon as the hostbuild itself reached `state: "installed"` -- but that file is assembled by a separate, asynchronous server-side step (ADR-0057), and the on-disk copy could be a stale leftover from an earlier round still finishing (or having failed) while `--deploy` happily redeployed it as if it were fresh. Reproduced live on 192.168.15.95 during task #672's own deploy verification.

Fixed: three new process-lifetime counters in `daemon/src/main.c` (`g_bootroot_assembly_started`/`_completed`/`_running`) -- `_completed` only ever advances on a real, confirmed assembly success (`WIFEXITED && WEXITSTATUS==0`), never on failure. Reported via `GET /system/boot` (not folded into `pkg.c`'s own generic hostbuild status JSON -- `pkg.c` stays agnostic to what "kanxeo" means, matching ADR-0057's own established separation; `GET /system/boot` is already the natural home for "state about the currently/most-recently-assembled boot image"). `cli/src/main.c`'s `cmd_pkg_hostbuild()` captures the completed generation as a baseline *before* a new "kanxeo" hostbuild round is even triggered (capturing it any later would itself race an earlier round's still-finishing assembly), then, once the hostbuild itself reaches `installed`, the new `wait_for_fresh_bootroot_assembly()` polls `GET /system/boot` until the generation advances past that baseline -- or reports a real, specific failure (pointing at `GET /system/logs`) if the assembly resolves without ever advancing past it, rather than spinning forever or trusting file-exists. Full clean rebuild (`-Wall -Werror`) zero warnings; full regression sweep (21 daemon-linked tests) clean. No new persisted state -- process-lifetime only, matching every other async-job tracking mechanism this daemon already has. See ADR-0105.

## Part 51 (done, full clean rebuild + full regression sweep): DELETE /containers finally removes its own on-disk state (ADR-0106, closes task #738)

`DELETE /v1/containers/{name}` only ever called `registry_remove()`/`containerdef_remove()` -- neither touches the filesystem, so a deleted container's own `upper`/`work`/`merged` directories leaked forever, a real disk-space leak on every deletion. `daemon/include/quotamap.h`'s own doc comment had cited this as a deliberate tradeoff ("ADR-0054's pre-existing backup/restore design") -- checked directly per this project's "diagnose before working around" discipline and confirmed stale/incorrect: ADR-0054 is entirely about host-side stats and says nothing about backup/restore; the real backup/restore ADR (ADR-0033) explicitly scopes workload data as out of its purview and reconstructs a restored container via fresh `containerdef` replay, never by resurrecting old upperdir content. No real design anywhere depended on the retention; it was a genuine oversight with an incorrect citation attached.
